Operations for Cleaning\n```python\n# Replace commas with dots (European decimal format)\ndf['column'] = df['column'].astype(str).str.replace(',', '.')\n\n# Remove whitespace\ndf['column'] = This skill covers common unit conversions for laboratory tests, particularly for kidney disease (CKD) markers.\n\n## Common Unit Systems\n\n### US Conventional Units vs. SI Units\n- **US Conventional Units**: Traditional units used in the United States (mg/dL, g/dL, etc.)\n- **SI Units (International System)**: Used in most other countries (mmol/L, µmol/L, g/L, etc.)\n\n## Kidney Function Markers\n\n### Serum Creatinine\n**US Conventional**: mg/dL\n**SI Unit**: µmol/L\n**Conversion**:\n- µmol/L to mg/dL: divide by 88.4\n- mg/dL to µmol/L: multiply by 88.4\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**:\n- Men: 0.7-1.3 mg/dL\n- Women: 0.6-1.1 mg/dL\n- CKD patients: typically elevated (1.5-15+ mg/dL depending on stage)\n\n```python\ndef convert_creatinine_to_mgdl(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert creatinine from µmol/L to mg/dL if needed\"\"\"\n    if value > 20:  # Likely in µmol/L (>20 is abnormal in mg/dL)\n        return value / 88.4\n    return value\n```\n\n### Blood Urea Nitrogen (BUN)\n**US Conventional**: mg/dL\n**SI Unit**: mmol/L\n**Conversion**:\n- mmol/L to mg/dL: multiply by 2.8\n- mg/dL to mmol/L: divide by 2.8\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**: 7-20 mg/dL\n\n```python\ndef convert_bun_to_mgdl(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert BUN from mmol/L to mg/dL if needed\"\"\"\n    if value < 3:  # Likely in mmol/L (normal range 2.5-7.1 mmol/L)\n        return value * 2.8\n    return value\n```\n\n### Cystatin C\n**US Conventional**: mg/L\n**SI Unit**: mg/L (same)\n**Normal Range**: 0.5-1.0 mg/L\n\nNo conversion needed - same units globally.\n\n### Uric Acid\n**US Conventional**: mg/dL\n**SI Unit**: µmol/L\n**Conversion**:\n- µmol/L to mg/dL: divide by 59.48\n- mg/dL to µmol/L: multiply by 59.48\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**:\n- Men: 3.4-7.0 mg/dL\n- Women: 2.4-6.0 mg/dL\n\n```python\ndef convert_uric_acid_to_mgdl(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert uric acid from µmol/L to mg/dL if needed\"\"\"\n    if value > 25:  # Likely in µmol/L\n        return value / 59.48\n    return value\n```\n\n## Electrolytes\n\n### Sodium, Potassium, Chloride\n**Units**: mEq/L (US) = mmol/L (SI) - **Same value**\n**Normal Ranges (US Conventional)**:\n- Sodium: 136-145 mEq/L\n- Potassium: 3.5-5.0 mEq/L\n- Chloride: 98-106 mEq/L\n\nNo conversion needed.\n\n### Bicarbonate\n**Units**: mEq/L (US) = mmol/L (SI) - **Same value**\n**Normal Range**: 22-28 mEq/L\n\n### Magnesium\n**US Conventional**: mg/dL or mEq/L\n**SI Unit**: mmol/L\n**Conversion**:\n- mmol/L to mg/dL: multiply by 2.43\n- mg/dL to mmol/L: divide by 2.43\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**: 1.7-2.2 mg/dL\n\n```python\ndef convert_magnesium_to_mgdl(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert magnesium from mmol/L to mg/dL if needed\"\"\"\n    if value < 1:  # Likely in mmol/L (normal 0.7-1.0 mmol/L)\n        return value * 2.43\n    return value\n```\n\n### Calcium\n**US Conventional**: mg/dL\n**SI Unit**: mmol/L\n**Conversion**:\n- mmol/L to mg/dL: multiply by 4.0\n- mg/dL to mmol/L: divide by 4.0\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**: 8.5-10.2 mg/dL\n\n```python\ndef convert_calcium_to_mgdl(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert calcium from mmol/L to mg/dL if needed\"\"\"\n    if value < 4:  # Likely in mmol/L (normal 2.1-2.6 mmol/L)\n        return value * 4.0\n    return value\n```\n\n### Phosphorus\n**US Conventional**: mg/dL\n**SI Unit**: mmol/L\n**Conversion**:\n- mmol/L to mg/dL: multiply by 3.1\n- mg/dL to mmol/L: divide by 3.1\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**: 2.5-4.5 mg/dL\n\n```python\ndef convert_phosphorus_to_mgdl(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert phosphorus from mmol/L to mg/dL if needed\"\"\"\n    if value < 2:  # Likely in mmol/L (normal 0.8-1.5 mmol/L)\n        return value * 3.1\n    return value\n```\n\n## Hematology\n\n### Hemoglobin\n**US Conventional**: g/dL\n**SI Unit**: g/L\n**Conversion**:\n- g/L to g/dL: divide by 10\n- g/dL to g/L: multiply by 10\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**:\n- Men: 13.5-17.5 g/dL\n- Women: 12.0-15.5 g/dL\n- CKD patients: often 9-12 g/dL\n\n```python\ndef convert_hemoglobin_to_gdl(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert hemoglobin from g/L to g/dL if needed\"\"\"\n    if value > 20:  # Likely in g/L (normal 120-170 g/L)\n        return value / 10\n    return value\n```\n\n### Hematocrit\n**Units**: % (percentage) - **Same globally**\n**Normal Range**:\n- Men: 38.8-50.0%\n- Women: 34.9-44.5%\n\n### RBC Count\n**US Conventional**: million cells/µL\n**SI Unit**: × 10^12 cells/L\n**Same numerical value** in practice\n\n**Normal Range**: 4.0-6.0 million/µL\n\n### WBC Count\n**US Conventional**: thousand cells/µL\n**SI Unit**: × 10^9 cells/L\n**Same numerical value** in practice\n\n**Normal Range**: 4.5-11.0 thousand/µL\n\n### Platelet Count\n**US Conventional**: thousand/µL\n**SI Unit**: × 10^9/L\n**Same numerical value** in practice\n\n**Normal Range**: 150-400 thousand/µL\n\n## Iron Studies\n\n### Serum Iron\n**US Conventional**: µg/dL\n**SI Unit**: µmol/L\n**Conversion**:\n- µmol/L to µg/dL: multiply by 5.58\n- µg/dL to µmol/L: divide by 5.58\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**: 60-170 µg/dL\n\n```python\ndef convert_iron_to_ugdl(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert serum iron from µmol/L to µg/dL if needed\"\"\"\n    if value < 50:  # Likely in µmol/L (normal 10-30 µmol/L)\n        return value * 5.58\n    return value\n```\n\n### TIBC (Total Iron Binding Capacity)\n**US Conventional**: µg/dL\n**SI Unit**: µmol/L\n**Conversion**: Same as serum iron (multiply by 5.58 or divide by 5.58)\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**: 250-450 µg/dL\n\n### Transferrin Saturation\n**Units**: % (percentage) - **Same globally**\n**Normal Range**: 20-50%\n\n### Ferritin\n**Units**: ng/mL (US) = µg/L (SI) - **Same value**\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**:\n- Men: 24-336 ng/mL\n- Women: 11-307 ng/mL\n- CKD patients: target 100-500 ng/mL for anemia management\n\n## Proteins\n\n### Albumin (Serum)\n**US Conventional**: g/dL\n**SI Unit**: g/L\n**Conversion**:\n- g/L to g/dL: divide by 10\n- g/dL to g/L: multiply by 10\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**: 3.5-5.5 g/dL\n\n```python\ndef convert_albumin_to_gdl(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert albumin from g/L to g/dL if needed\"\"\"\n    if value > 20:  # Likely in g/L (normal 35-55 g/L)\n        return value / 10\n    return value\n```\n\n### Total Protein\n**US Conventional**: g/dL\n**SI Unit**: g/L\n**Conversion**: Same as albumin (divide by 10 or multiply by 10)\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**: 6.0-8.3 g/dL\n\n## Lipids\n\n### Total Cholesterol, LDL, HDL\n**US Conventional**: mg/dL\n**SI Unit**: mmol/L\n**Conversion**:\n- mmol/L to mg/dL: multiply by 38.67\n- mg/dL to mmol/L: divide by 38.67\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**:\n- Total Cholesterol: <200 mg/dL\n- LDL: <100 mg/dL\n- HDL: >40 mg/dL (men), >50 mg/dL (women)\n\n```python\ndef convert_cholesterol_to_mgdl(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert cholesterol from mmol/L to mg/dL if needed\"\"\"\n    if value < 15:  # Likely in mmol/L (normal <5.2 mmol/L)\n        return value * 38.67\n    return value\n```\n\n### Triglycerides\n**US Conventional**: mg/dL\n**SI Unit**: mmol/L\n**Conversion**:\n- mmol/L to mg/dL: multiply by 88.57\n- mg/dL to mmol/L: divide by 88.57\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**: <150 mg/dL\n\n```python\ndef convert_triglycerides_to_mgdl(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert triglycerides from mmol/L to mg/dL if needed\"\"\"\n    if value < 10:  # Likely in mmol/L (normal <1.7 mmol/L)\n        return value * 88.57\n    return value\n```\n\n## Glucose Metabolism\n\n### Glucose\n**US Conventional**: mg/dL\n**SI Unit**: mmol/L\n**Conversion**:\n- mmol/L to mg/dL: multiply by 18.0\n- mg/dL to mmol/L: divide by 18.0\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**: 70-100 mg/dL (fasting)\n\n```python\ndef convert_glucose_to_mgdl(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert glucose from mmol/L to mg/dL if needed\"\"\"\n    if value < 30:  # Likely in mmol/L (normal 3.9-5.6 mmol/L)\n        return value * 18.0\n    return value\n```\n\n### HbA1c\n**US Conventional**: % (percentage)\n**Alternative**: mmol/mol (IFCC units)\n**Conversion**:\n- mmol/mol to %: (mmol/mol / 10.929) + 2.15\n- % to mmol/mol: (% - 2.15) × 10.929\n\n**Normal Range (US Conventional)**: <5.7%\n\n```python\ndef convert_hba1c_to_percent(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert HbA1c from mmol/mol to % if needed\"\"\"\n    if value > 25:  # Likely in mmol/mol (normal <39 mmol/mol)\n        return (value / 10.929) + 2.15\n    return value\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Always check physiological plausibility**: If a value seems impossible, it's likely in the wrong unit\n2. **Use range-based detection**: Values outside expected ranges are often in alternative units\n3. **Document conversions**: Keep track of which values were converted\n4. **Validate after conversion**: Ensure converted values fall within expected ranges\n5. **Consider clinical context**: Age, sex, and disease state affect normal ranges\n6. **Handle edge cases**: Very high or very low values might be valid, not unit errors\n\n## Example: Comprehensive Unit Harmonization Function\n```python\ndef harmonize_lab_value(value, test_name, us_min, us_max, conversion_factor):\n    \"\"\"\n    Harmonize lab value to US conventional units\n\n    Args:\n        value: The lab value\n        test_name: Name of the test\n        us_min: Minimum expected value in US units\n        us_max: Maximum expected value in US units (liberal range for sick patients)\n        conversion_factor: Factor to convert from SI to US units\n\n    Returns:\n        Converted value in US conventional units\n    \"\"\"\n    # If value is already in reasonable range, no conversion needed\n    if us_min <= value <= us_max:\n        return value\n\n    # Try converting\n    converted = value * conversion_factor if value < us_min else value / conversion_factor\n\n    # True\nis_scientific_notation(\"123.45\")  # False\nis_scientific_notation(\"1.23E+05\")  # True\n```\n\n### Suppressing Scientific Notation in Output\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Suppress scientific notation in pandas display\npd.options.display.float_format = '{:.2f}'.format\n\n# Suppress scientific notation in numpy\nnp.set_printoptions(suppress=True)\n\n# For a single number\nvalue = 1.23e2\nformatted = f'{value:.2f}'  # 12.34\n\n# Pandas Series\ndf['column'] = df['column'].astype(str).str.replace(',', '.')\ndf['column'] = pd.to_numeric(df['column'], errors='coerce')\n\n# Handle both formats (robust approach)\ndef parse_decimal(value_str):\n    \"\"\"Parse decimal in either European or American format\"\"\"\n    value_str = str(value_str).strip()\n\n    # Count commas and periods\n    comma_count = value_str.count(',')\n    period_count = value_str.count('.')\n\n    # If both present, assume thousands separator and decimal\n    if comma_count > 0 and period_count > 0:\n        # Determine which is decimal separator\n        comma_pos = value_str.rfind(',')\n        period_pos = value_str.rfind('.')\n\n        if comma_pos > period_pos:\n            # Comma is decimal (European with period as thousands)\n            value_str = value_str.replace('.', '').replace(',', '.')\n        else:\n            # Period is decimal (American with comma as thousands)\n            value_str = value_str.replace(',', '')\n\n    # Only comma (European decimal)\n    elif comma_count > 0:\n        value_str = value_str.replace(',', '.')\n\n    # Only period or neither - already American format\n    return float(value_str)\n\n# Examples\nparse_decimal(\"12,34\")        # 12.34\nparse_decimal(\"12.34\")        # 12.34\nparse_decimal(\"1,234.56\")     # 1234.56\nparse_decimal(\"1.234,56\")     # 1234.56\n```\n\n## Number Formatting\n\n### Formatting to Fixed Decimal Places\n```python\n# Using f-strings (Python 3.6+)\nvalue = 123.456789\nformatted = f'{value:.2f}'  # \"123.46\" (ensures 2 decimals even for round numbers)\n\n# Force 2 decimals even for integers\ninteger_value = 123\nformatted = f'{integer_value:.2f}'  # \"123.00\"\n```\n\n### Formatting with Leading Zeros\n```python\n# Integer with leading zeros\nvalue = 42\nformatted = f'{value:05d}'  # 123.46\n\n# Always round up\nmath.ceil(value * 100) / 100  # 123.46\n\n# Always round down\nmath.floor(value * 100) / 100  # 123.45\n\n# Truncate (remove decimals)\nmath.trunc(value)  # 123\n```\n\n## Pandas-Specific Formatting\n\n### Format Entire DataFrame\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# Round all numeric columns to 2 decimals\ndf_rounded = df.round(2)\n\n# Format all numeric columns as strings with 2 decimals\nfor col in df.select_dtypes(include=[np.number]).columns:\n    df[col] = df[col].apply(lambda x: f'{x:.2f}' if pd.notna(x) else x)\n\n# Apply formatting function\ndef format_decimal(x):\n    if pd.isna(x):\n        return x\n    return f'{float(x):.2f}'\n\ndf['column'] = df['column'].apply(format_decimal)\n```\n\n### Format Specific Columns\n```python\n# Format selected columns\ncols_to_format = ['col1', 'col2', 'col3']\nfor col in cols_to_format:\n    df[col] = df[col].apply(lambda x: f'{x:.2f}' if pd.notna(x) else x)\n\n# Using applymap (deprecated in newer pandas, use map instead)\ndf[cols_to_format] = df[cols_to_format].map(lambda x: f'{x:.2f}' if pd.notna(x) else x)\n```\n\n## Handling Special Cases\n\n### Dealing with NaN and None\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef safe_format(value, decimals=2):\n    \"\"\"Format number safely, handling NaN and None\"\"\"\n    if pd.isna(value) or value is None:\n        return value  # or return '' or 'N/A'\n    try:\n        return f'{float(value):.{decimals}f}'\n    except (ValueError, TypeError):\n        return value  # Return original if conversion fails\n\n# Usage\ndf['column'] = df['column'].apply(safe_format)\n```\n\n### Handling Very Large and Very Small Numbers\n```python\ndef format_number_smart(value, decimals=2):\n    \"\"\"Format number, using scientific notation for very large/small values\"\"\"\n    if pd.isna(value):\n        return value\n\n    abs_val = abs(value)\n\n    # Use scientific notation for very large or very small\n    if abs_val >= 1e6 or (abs_val < 0.01 and abs_val != 0):\n        return f'{value:.{decimals}e}'\n    else:\n        return f'{value:.{decimals}f}'\n\n# Examples\nformat_number_smart(1234567)      # \"1.23e+06\"\nformat_number_smart(0.00123)      # \"1.23e-03\"\nformat_number_smart(123.456)      # \"123.46\"\n```\n\n### Preserving Significant Figures\n```python\ndef format_sig_figs(value, sig_figs=3):\n    \"\"\"Format number to specified significant figures\"\"\"\n    if value == 0:\n        return f'{0:.{sig_figs-1}f}'\n\n    from math import log10, floor\n\n    # Calculate order of magnitude\n    magnitude = floor(log10(abs(value)))\n\n    # Calculate decimals needed\n    decimals = sig_figs - magnitude - 1\n\n    if decimals < 0:\n        decimals = 0\n\n    return f'{value:.{decimals}f}'\n\n# Examples\nformat_sig_figs(123.456, 3)   # \"123\"\nformat_sig_figs(0.00123, 3)   # \"0.00123\"\nformat_sig_figs(12345, 3)     # \"12300\"\n```\n\n## String Cleaning\n\n### Remove Unwanted Characters\n```python\n# Remove whitespace\ncleaned = \"  123.45  \".strip()  # Determine columns to process\n    if exclude_cols is None:\n        exclude_cols = []\n\n    cols_to_process = [col for col in df_clean.columns if col not in exclude_cols]\n\n    # Step 1: Convert to string to handle mixed formats\n    for col in cols_to_process:\n        df_clean[col] = df_clean[col].astype(str)\n\n    # 123.0\n\n# From pandas Series\nimport pandas as pd\ndf['column'] = df['column'].astype(float)\n```\n\n### Detecting Scientific Notation in Strings\n```python\ndef is_scientific_notation(s):\n    \"\"\"Check if string is in scientific notation\"\"\"\n    s = str(s).lower()\n    return 'e' in s or 'e+' in s or 'e-' in s\n\n# Examples\nis_scientific_notation(\"1.23e2\")  # True\nis_scientific_notation(\"123.45\")  # False\nis_scientific_notation(\"1.23E+05\")  # True\n```\n\n### Suppressing Scientific Notation in Output\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Suppress scientific notation in pandas display\npd.options.display.float_format = '{:.2f}'.format\n\n# Suppress scientific notation in numpy\nnp.set_printoptions(suppress=True)\n\n# For a single number\nvalue = 1.23e2\nformatted = f'{value:.2f}'  # \"123.00\"\n```\n\n## Decimal Format Conversions\n\n### European vs. American Decimal Format\n- **European**: Uses comma as decimal separator (e.g., `12,34` = twelve point three four)\n- **American**: Uses period/dot as decimal separator (e.g., `12.34`)\n\n### Converting European to American Format\n```python\n# Single value\neuropean_num = \"12,34\"\namerican_num = european_num.replace(',', '.')\nvalue = float(american_num)  # 12.34\n\n# Pandas Series\ndf['column'] = df['column'].astype(str).str.replace(',', '.')\ndf['column'] = pd.to_numeric(df['column'], errors='coerce')\n\n# Handle both formats (robust approach)\ndef parse_decimal(value_str):\n    \"\"\"Parse decimal in either European or American format\"\"\"\n    value_str = str(value_str).strip()\n\n    # Count commas and periods\n    comma_count = value_str.count(',')\n    period_count = value_str.count('.')\n\n    # If both present, assume thousands separator and decimal\n    if comma_count > 0 and period_count > 0:\n        # Determine which is decimal separator\n        comma_pos = value_str.rfind(',')\n        period_pos = value_str.rfind('.')\n\n        if comma_pos > period_pos:\n            # Comma is decimal (European with period as thousands)\n            value_str = value_str.replace('.', '').replace(',', '.')\n        else:\n            # Period is decimal (American with comma as thousands)\n            value_str = value_str.replace(',', '')\n\n    # Only comma (European decimal)\n    elif comma_count > 0:\n        value_str = value_str.replace(',', '.')\n\n    # Only period or neither - already American format\n    return float(value_str)\n\n# Examples\nparse_decimal(\"12,34\")        # 12.34\nparse_decimal(\"12.34\")        # 12.34\nparse_decimal(\"1,234.56\")     # 1234.56\nparse_decimal(\"1.234,56\")     # 1234.56\n```\n\n## Number Formatting\n\n### Formatting to Fixed Decimal Places\n```python\n# Using f-strings (Python 3.6+)\nvalue = 123.456789\nformatted = f'{value:.2f}'  # \"123.46\" (rounded)\n\n# Using format() method\nformatted = '{:.2f}'.format(value)  # \"123.46\"\n\n# Using round() function\nrounded = round(value, 2)  # 123.46 (numeric, not string)\nformatted = f'{rounded:.2f}'  # \"123.46\" (ensures 2 decimals even for round numbers)\n\n# Force 2 decimals even for integers\ninteger_value = 123\nformatted = f'{integer_value:.2f}'  # \"123.00\"\n```\n\n### Formatting with Leading Zeros\n```python\n# Integer with leading zeros\nvalue = 42\nformatted = f'{value:05d}'  # \"00042\"\n\n# Float with fixed width and decimals\nvalue = 12.3\nformatted = f'{value:08.2f}'  # \"00012.30\"\n```\n\n### Rounding Methods\n```python\nimport math\n\nvalue = 123.456\n\n# Standard rounding (round half to even)\nround(value, 2)  # 123.46\n\n# Always round up\nmath.ceil(value * 100) / 100  # 123.46\n\n# Always round down\nmath.floor(value * 100) / 100  # 123.45\n\n# Truncate (remove decimals)\nmath.trunc(value)  # 123\n```\n\n## Pandas-Specific Formatting\n\n### Format Entire DataFrame\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# Round all numeric columns to 2 decimals\ndf_rounded = df.round(2)\n\n# Format all numeric columns as strings with 2 decimals\nfor col in df.select_dtypes(include=[np.number]).columns:\n    df[col] = df[col].apply(lambda x: f'{x:.2f}' if pd.notna(x) else x)\n\n# Apply formatting function\ndef format_decimal(x):\n    if pd.isna(x):\n        return x\n    return f'{float(x):.2f}'\n\ndf['column'] = df['column'].apply(format_decimal)\n```\n\n### Format Specific Columns\n```python\n# Format selected columns\ncols_to_format = ['col1', 'col2', 'col3']\nfor col in cols_to_format:\n    df[col] = df[col].apply(lambda x: f'{x:.2f}' if pd.notna(x) else x)\n\n# Using applymap (deprecated in newer pandas, use map instead)\ndf[cols_to_format] = df[cols_to_format].map(lambda x: f'{x:.2f}' if pd.notna(x) else x)\n```\n\n## Handling Special Cases\n\n### Dealing with NaN and None\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef safe_format(value, decimals=2):\n    \"\"\"Format number safely, handling NaN and None\"\"\"\n    if pd.isna(value) or value is None:\n        return value  # or return '' or 'N/A'\n    try:\n        return f'{float(value):.{decimals}f}'\n    except (ValueError, TypeError):\n        return value  # Return original if conversion fails\n\n# Usage\ndf['column'] = df['column'].apply(safe_format)\n```\n\n### Handling Very Large and Very Small Numbers\n```python\ndef format_number_smart(value, decimals=2):\n    \"\"\"Format number, using scientific notation for very large/small values\"\"\"\n    if pd.isna(value):\n        return value\n\n    abs_val = abs(value)\n\n    # Use scientific notation for very large or very small\n    if abs_val >= 1e6 or (abs_val < 0.01 and abs_val != 0):\n        return f'{value:.{decimals}e}'\n    else:\n        return f'{value:.{decimals}f}'\n\n# Examples\nformat_number_smart(1234567)      # \"1.23e+06\"\nformat_number_smart(0.00123)      # \"1.23e-03\"\nformat_number_smart(123.456)      # \"123.46\"\n```\n\n### Preserving Significant Figures\n```python\ndef format_sig_figs(value, sig_figs=3):\n    \"\"\"Format number to specified significant figures\"\"\"\n    if value == 0:\n        return f'{0:.{sig_figs-1}f}'\n\n    from math import log10, floor\n\n    # Calculate order of magnitude\n    magnitude = floor(log10(abs(value)))\n\n    # Calculate decimals needed\n    decimals = sig_figs - magnitude - 1\n\n    if decimals < 0:\n        decimals = 0\n\n    return f'{value:.{decimals}f}'\n\n# Examples\nformat_sig_figs(123.456, 3)   # \"123\"\nformat_sig_figs(0.00123, 3)   # \"0.00123\"\nformat_sig_figs(12345, 3)     # \"12300\"\n```\n\n## String Cleaning\n\n### Remove Unwanted Characters\n```python\n# Remove whitespace\ncleaned = \"  123.45  \".strip()  # \"123.45\"\n\n# Remove specific characters\nvalue = \"$123.45\"\ncleaned = value.replace('$', '')  # \"123.45\"\n\n# Remove multiple characters\nvalue = \"$1,234.56\"\ncleaned = value.replace('$', '').replace(',', '')  # \"1234.56\"\n\n# Using regex\nimport re\nvalue = \"$1,234.56 USD\"\ncleaned = re.sub(r'[^0-9.]', '', value)  # \"1234.56\"\n```\n\n### Pandas String Cleaning\n```python\n# Remove characters from entire column\ndf['column'] = df['column'].str.replace(',', '.', regex=False)\ndf['column'] = df['column'].str.replace(r'[^0-9.]', '', regex=True)\n\n# Chain operations\ndf['column'] = (df['column']\n                .str.strip()\n                .str.replace(',', '.')\n                .str.replace(r'[^0-9.eE+-]', '', regex=True))\n```\n\n## Complete Example: Cleaning Numeric Data\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef clean_and_format_numeric(df, exclude_cols=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Clean and format numeric data in a DataFrame\n\n    Args:\n        df: Input DataFrame\n        exclude_cols: List of column names to exclude (e.g., ID columns)\n\n    Returns:\n        Cleaned DataFrame with formatted values\n    \"\"\"\n    df_clean = df.copy()\n\n    # Step 6: Format as strings with exactly 2 decimal places\n    for col in cols_to_process:\n        df_clean[col] = df_clean[col].apply(lambda x: f'{x:.2f}' if pd.notna(x) else x)\n\n    return df_clean\n\n# Usage\ndf_formatted = clean_and_format_numeric(df, exclude_cols=['patient_id'])\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Always validate after conversion**: Check for NaN values that might indicate failed conversions\n2. **Handle edge cases**: Consider empty strings, None, NaN, inf\n3. **Preserve precision**: Be aware that floating-point arithmetic can introduce small errors\n4. **Test with sample data**: Verify your formatting works with various input formats\n5. **Document assumptions**: Make clear what input formats are expected\n6. **Use try-except for robustness**: Catch conversion errors gracefully\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n1. **Floating-point precision**: `0.1 + 0.2 != 0.3` in binary floating-point\n2. **Rounding errors**: Multiple rounding operations can accumulate errors\n3. **Scientific notation creep**: Very large/small numbers may revert to scientific notation\n4. **Locale-specific formatting**: Different regions use different formats\n5. **String vs. numeric**: Remember that formatted strings can't be used in calculations\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Missing Data\n**Types of Missing Data:**\n- **Missing Completely at Random (MCAR)**: No pattern to missingness\n- **Missing at Random (MAR)**: Missingness depends on observed data\n- **Missing Not at Random (MNAR)**: Missingness depends on unobserved data\n\n**Handling Strategies:**\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# Identify missing patterns\nmissing_summary = df.isnull().sum()\nmissing_pct = (df.isnull().sum() / len(df)) * 100\n\n# Remove rows with any missing values\ndf_complete = df.dropna()\n\n# Remove rows with missing in critical columns\ncritical_cols = ['patient_id', 'creatinine', 'eGFR']\ndf_clean = df.dropna(subset=critical_cols)\n\n# Remove columns with high missingness (>50%)\nthreshold = 0.5\ndf_clean = df.loc[:, df.isnull().mean() < threshold]\n```\n\n### 2. Out-of-Range Values\n**Physiological Impossibilities:**\n- Negative values where only positive is possible (e.g., hemoglobin)\n- Values exceeding biological maximum (e.g., creatinine > 30 mg/dL unlikely)\n- Zero values where biologically impossible (e.g., body temperature)\n\n```python\ndef identify_outliers(df, column, min_val, max_val):\n    \"\"\"Identify values outside physiological range\"\"\"\n    out_of_range = df[(df[column] < min_val) | (df[column] > max_val)]\n    return out_of_range\n\n# Example\noutliers = identify_outliers(df, 'hemoglobin', 3, 20)  # g/dL\nprint(f\"Found {len(outliers)} outliers in hemoglobin\")\n```\n\n### 4. Unit Inconsistencies\n**Detection Approaches:**\n- Statistical analysis (mean, median, distribution)\n- Range-based detection (values outside expected range likely wrong unit)\n- Clustering (bimodal distributions suggest mixed units)\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef detect_unit_inconsistency(series, expected_min, expected_max):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect potential unit inconsistencies\n\n    Returns indices of values likely in wrong units\n    \"\"\"\n    # Values too low or too high\n    too_low = series < (expected_min / 10)\n    too_high = series > (expected_max * 10)\n\n    inconsistent = too_low | too_high\n    return series[inconsistent].index.tolist()\n\n# Example: Detect creatinine in µmol/L when mg/dL expected\ninconsistent_idx = detect_unit_inconsistency(df['Serum_Creatinine'], 0.5, 15)\nprint(f\"Found {len(inconsistent_idx)} values likely in wrong units\")\n```\n\n## Data Validation Rules\n\n### Range Validation\n```python\n# Define physiological ranges for CKD patients (liberal ranges)\nvalidation_ranges = {\n    'Serum_Creatinine': (0.3, 20.0),      # mg/dL\n    'BUN': (5, 200),                       # mg/dL\n    'eGFR': (1, 200),                      # mL/min/1.73m²\n    'Hemoglobin': (3, 20),                 # g/dL\n    'Sodium': (110, 165),                  # mEq/L\n    'Potassium': (2.0, 8.0),               # mEq/L\n    'Glucose': (20, 800),                  # mg/dL\n    'Albumin_Serum': (1.0, 6.0),          # g/dL\n}\n\ndef validate_ranges(df, ranges):\n    \"\"\"\n    Validate that values fall within expected ranges\n\n    Returns DataFrame with validation results\n    \"\"\"\n    validation_results = pd.DataFrame()\n\n    for col, (min_val, max_val) in ranges.items():\n        if col in df.columns:\n            valid = (df[col] >= min_val) & (df[col] <= max_val)\n            validation_results[f'{col}_valid'] = valid\n            invalid_count = (~valid).sum()\n            print(f\"{col}: {invalid_count} invalid values\")\n\n    return validation_results\n```\n\n### Logical Consistency Checks\n```python\ndef check_logical_consistency(df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Check for logical inconsistencies in lab data\n\n    Returns list of inconsistency descriptions\n    \"\"\"\n    issues = []\n\n    # eGFR should be inversely related to creatinine\n    # High creatinine should mean low eGFR\n    if 'eGFR' in df.columns and 'Serum_Creatinine' in df.columns:\n        high_cre_high_gfr = df[(df['Serum_Creatinine'] > 2.0) & (df['eGFR'] > 60)]\n        if len(high_cre_high_gfr) > 0:\n            issues.append(f\"{len(high_cre_high_gfr)} patients with high creatinine but high eGFR\")\n\n    # BUN/Creatinine ratio should be reasonable (typically 10-20)\n    if 'BUN_Creatinine_Ratio' in df.columns:\n        abnormal_ratio = df[(df['BUN_Creatinine_Ratio'] < 5) | (df['BUN_Creatinine_Ratio'] > 50)]\n        if len(abnormal_ratio) > 0:\n            issues.append(f\"{len(abnormal_ratio)} patients with abnormal BUN/Cr ratio\")\n\n    # Hematocrit should be ~3× hemoglobin\n    if 'Hemoglobin' in df.columns and 'Hematocrit' in df.columns:\n        expected_hct = df['Hemoglobin'] * 3\n        hct_mismatch = abs(df['Hematocrit'] - expected_hct) > 10\n        if hct_mismatch.sum() > 0:\n            issues.append(f\"{hct_mismatch.sum()} patients with Hct/Hgb mismatch\")\n\n    # Total cholesterol should be approximately LDL + HDL + (TG/5)\n    if all(col in df.columns for col in ['Total_Cholesterol', 'LDL_Cholesterol', 'HDL_Cholesterol', 'Triglycerides']):\n        calculated_total = df['LDL_Cholesterol'] + df['HDL_Cholesterol'] + (df['Triglycerides'] / 5)\n        total_mismatch = abs(df['Total_Cholesterol'] - calculated_total) > 50\n        if total_mismatch.sum() > 0:\n            issues.append(f\"{total_mismatch.sum()} patients with cholesterol calculation mismatch\")\n\n    return issues\n```\n\n## Data Quality Metrics\n\n### Completeness\n```python\ndef calculate_completeness(df):\n    \"\"\"Calculate completeness metrics\"\"\"\n    total_cells = df.size\n    missing_cells = df.isnull().sum().sum()\n    completeness = (1 - missing_cells / total_cells) * 100\n\n    return {\n        'overall_completeness': completeness,\n        'complete_rows': (df.notna().all(axis=1).sum() / len(df)) * 100,\n        'columns_completeness': ((1 - df.isnull().sum() / len(df)) * 100).to_dict()\n    }\n```\n\n### Validity\n```python\ndef calculate_validity(df, validation_ranges):\n    \"\"\"Calculate percentage of valid values per column\"\"\"\n    validity_scores = {}\n\n    for col, (min_val, max_val) in validation_ranges.items():\n        if col in df.columns:\n            valid_count = ((df[col] >= min_val) & (df[col] <= max_val)).sum()\n            non_null_count = df[col].notna().sum()\n            if non_null_count > 0:\n                validity_scores[col] = (valid_count / non_null_count) * 100\n\n    return validity_scores\n```\n\n### Consistency\n```python\ndef calculate_consistency(df):\n    \"\"\"Calculate consistency metrics\"\"\"\n    consistency_metrics = {}\n\n    # Example: Check if BUN/Creatinine ratio matches calculated ratio\n    if all(col in df.columns for col in ['BUN', 'Serum_Creatinine', 'BUN_Creatinine_Ratio']):\n        calculated_ratio = df['BUN'] / df['Serum_Creatinine']\n        consistent = abs(df['BUN_Creatinine_Ratio'] - calculated_ratio) < 1\n        consistency_metrics['BUN_Cr_ratio'] = (consistent.sum() / len(df)) * 100\n\n    return consistency_metrics\n```\n\n## Clinical Lab Value Plausibility\n\n### CKD-Specific Validation\n```python\ndef validate_ckd_labs(df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Validate lab values specific to CKD patients\n    CKD patients have different \"normal\" ranges\n    \"\"\"\n    validation_results = {}\n\n    # In CKD, expect:\n    # - Elevated creatinine\n    # - Reduced eGFR\n    # - Often anemia (low hemoglobin)\n    # - Possibly elevated potassium, phosphorus\n    # - Possibly low calcium, bicarbonate\n\n    if 'eGFR' in df.columns and 'Serum_Creatinine' in df.columns:\n        # For CKD patients, eGFR should be <60\n        ckd_stage_3_plus = df['eGFR'] < 60\n        elevated_creatinine = df['Serum_Creatinine'] > 1.2\n\n        # These should generally align\n        aligned = ckd_stage_3_plus == elevated_creatinine\n        validation_results['eGFR_creatinine_aligned'] = (aligned.sum() / len(df)) * 100\n\n    # Check for anemia (common in CKD)\n    if 'Hemoglobin' in df.columns:\n        anemic = df['Hemoglobin'] < 13  # Simplified threshold\n        validation_results['anemia_prevalence'] = (anemic.sum() / len(df)) * 100\n\n    return validation_results\n```\n\n### Temporal Consistency (for longitudinal data)\n```python\ndef check_temporal_plausibility(df, date_col, value_col, max_change_per_day):\n    \"\"\"\n    Check if changes over time are physiologically plausible\n\n    Args:\n        df: DataFrame with datetime column and value column\n        date_col: Name of datetime column\n        value_col: Name of value column\n        max_change_per_day: Maximum plausible change per day\n    \"\"\"\n    df_sorted = df.sort_values(date_col)\n\n    # Calculate change per day\n    df_sorted['days_diff'] = df_sorted[date_col].diff().dt.days\n    df_sorted['value_diff'] = df_sorted[value_col].diff()\n    df_sorted['change_per_day'] = df_sorted['value_diff'] / df_sorted['days_diff']\n\n    # Identify implausible changes\n    implausible = abs(df_sorted['change_per_day']) > max_change_per_day\n\n    return df_sorted[implausible]\n```\n\n## Data Harmonization Quality Checks\n\n### Pre-Harmonization Assessment\n```python\ndef pre_harmonization_check(df, exclude_cols=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Assess data quality before harmonization\n\n    Returns dictionary with quality metrics\n    \"\"\"\n    if exclude_cols is None:\n        exclude_cols = []\n\n    metrics = {}\n\n    # Check for mixed decimal formats (commas vs periods)\n    for col in df.columns:\n        if col not in exclude_cols:\n            str_values = df[col].astype(str)\n            has_commas = str_values.str.contains(',', na=False).sum()\n            has_periods = str_values.str.contains(r'\\.', na=False, regex=True).sum()\n\n            if has_commas > 0 and has_periods > 0:\n                metrics[f'{col}_mixed_decimals'] = True\n\n    # Check for scientific notation\n    for col in df.columns:\n        if col not in exclude_cols:\n            str_values = df[col].astype(str)\n            scientific = str_values.str.contains('e|E', na=False, regex=True).sum()\n            metrics[f'{col}_scientific_notation_count'] = scientific\n\n    # Check for missing values\n    metrics['missing_by_column'] = df.isnull().sum().to_dict()\n    metrics['rows_with_missing'] = df.isnull().any(axis=1).sum()\n\n    return metrics\n```\n\n### Post-Harmonization Validation\n```python\ndef post_harmonization_check(df, expected_format='X.XX'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Validate data after harmonization\n\n    Args:\n        df: Harmonized DataFrame\n        expected_format: Expected format (e.g., 'X.XX' for 2 decimal places)\n    \"\"\"\n    issues = []\n\n    # Check all values are numeric (as strings)\n    for col in df.columns:\n        if col == 'patient_id':\n            continue\n\n        # Check format\n        str_values = df[col].astype(str)\n\n        # Check for scientific notation\n        scientific = str_values.str.contains('e|E', na=False, regex=True).sum()\n        if scientific > 0:\n            issues.append(f\"{col}: {scientific} values still in scientific notation\")\n\n        # Check for commas\n        commas = str_values.str.contains(',', na=False).sum()\n        if commas > 0:\n            issues.append(f\"{col}: {commas} values still have commas\")\n\n        # Check decimal places\n        if expected_format == 'X.XX':\n            # Should match pattern like \"12.34\"\n            import re\n            pattern = r'^\\d+\\.\\d{2}$'\n            correct_format = str_values.str.match(pattern, na=False).sum()\n            total_valid = df[col].notna().sum()\n\n            if correct_format < total_valid:\n                issues.append(f\"{col}: {total_valid - correct_format} values not in X.XX format\")\n\n    return issues\n```\n\n## Best Practices for Clinical Data Quality\n\n### 1. Always Validate Before and After Processing\n```python\n# Before processing\nprint(\"Pre-processing quality check:\")\npre_metrics = pre_harmonization_check(df)\n\n# Process data\ndf_clean = clean_data(df)\n\n# After processing\nprint(\"Post-processing quality check:\")\npost_issues = post_harmonization_check(df_clean)\n```\n\n### 2. Document All Quality Issues\n```python\ndef generate_quality_report(df, output_file='quality_report.txt'):\n    \"\"\"Generate comprehensive data quality report\"\"\"\n    with open(output_file, 'w') as f:\n        f.write(\"DATA QUALITY REPORT\\n\")\n        f.write(\"=\" * 50 + \"\\n\\n\")\n\n        # Completeness\n        f.write(\"COMPLETENESS\\n\")\n        f.write(\"-\" * 50 + \"\\n\")\n        completeness = calculate_completeness(df)\n        f.write(f\"Overall: {completeness['overall_completeness']:.2f}%\\n\")\n        f.write(f\"Complete rows: {completeness['complete_rows']:.2f}%\\n\\n\")\n\n        # Missing values by column\n        f.write(\"Missing values by column:\\n\")\n        for col, pct in completeness['columns_completeness'].items():\n            f.write(f\"  {col}: {100-pct:.2f}% missing\\n\")\n\n        # Add more sections as needed\n```\n\n### 3. Use Visualization for Quality Assessment\n```python\ndef visualize_data_quality(df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Create visualizations for data quality assessment\n    Requires matplotlib and seaborn\n    \"\"\"\n    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n    import seaborn as sns\n\n    # Missing data heatmap\n    plt.figure(figsize=(12, 8))\n    sns.heatmap(df.isnull(), cbar=False, yticklabels=False)\n    plt.title('Missing Data Pattern')\n    plt.savefig('missing_data_heatmap.png')\n\n    # Distribution of numeric columns\n    numeric_cols = df.select_dtypes(include=[np.number]).columns\n    fig, axes = plt.subplots(len(numeric_cols)//3 + 1, 3, figsize=(15, 10))\n    for i, col in enumerate(numeric_cols):\n        ax = axes[i//3, i%3]\n        df[col].hist(ax=ax, bins=50)\n        ax.set_title(col)\n    plt.tight_layout()\n    plt.savefig('distributions.png')\n```\n\n### 4. Missing Data\n**Types of Missing Data:**\n- **Missing Completely at Random (MCAR)**: No pattern to missingness\n- **Missing at Random (MAR)**: Missingness depends on observed data\n- **Missing Not at Random (MNAR)**: Missingness depends on unobserved data\n\n**Handling Strategies:**\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# Identify missing patterns\nmissing_summary = df.isnull().sum()\nmissing_pct = (df.isnull().sum() / len(df)) * 100\n\n# Remove rows with any missing values\ndf_complete = df.dropna()\n\n# Remove rows with missing in critical columns\ncritical_cols = ['patient_id', 'creatinine', 'eGFR']\ndf_clean = df.dropna(subset=critical_cols)\n\n# Remove columns with high missingness (>50%)\nthreshold = 0.5\ndf_clean = df.loc[:, df.isnull().mean() < threshold]\n```\n\n### 2. Duplicate Records\n```python\n# Check for duplicates\nduplicates = df[df.duplicated(subset=['patient_id'], keep=False)]\n\n# Remove duplicates, keeping first occurrence\ndf_unique = df.drop_duplicates(subset=['patient_id'], keep='first')\n\n# Remove complete duplicates (all columns identical)\ndf_unique = df.drop_duplicates()\n```\n\n### 3. Out-of-Range Values\n**Physiological Impossibilities:**\n- Negative values where only positive is possible (e.g., hemoglobin)\n- Values exceeding biological maximum (e.g., creatinine > 30 mg/dL unlikely)\n- Zero values where biologically impossible (e.g., body temperature)\n\n```python\ndef identify_outliers(df, column, min_val, max_val):\n    \"\"\"Identify values outside physiological range\"\"\"\n    out_of_range = df[(df[column] < min_val) | (df[column] > max_val)]\n    return out_of_range\n\n# Example\noutliers = identify_outliers(df, 'hemoglobin', 3, 20)  # g/dL\nprint(f\"Found {len(outliers)} outliers in hemoglobin\")\n```\n\n### 4. Unit Inconsistencies\n**Detection Approaches:**\n- Statistical analysis (mean, median, distribution)\n- Range-based detection (values outside expected range likely wrong unit)\n- Clustering (bimodal distributions suggest mixed units)\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef detect_unit_inconsistency(series, expected_min, expected_max):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect potential unit inconsistencies\n\n    Returns indices of values likely in wrong units\n    \"\"\"\n    # Values too low or too high\n    too_low = series < (expected_min / 10)\n    too_high = series > (expected_max * 10)\n\n    inconsistent = too_low | too_high\n    return series[inconsistent].index.tolist()\n\n# Example: Detect creatinine in µmol/L when mg/dL expected\ninconsistent_idx = detect_unit_inconsistency(df['Serum_Creatinine'], 0.5, 15)\nprint(f\"Found {len(inconsistent_idx)} values likely in wrong units\")\n```\n\n## Data Validation Rules\n\n### Range Validation\n```python\n# Define physiological ranges for CKD patients (liberal ranges)\nvalidation_ranges = {\n    'Serum_Creatinine': (0.3, 20.0),      # mg/dL\n    'BUN': (5, 200),                       # mg/dL\n    'eGFR': (1, 200),                      # mL/min/1.73m²\n    'Hemoglobin': (3, 20),                 # g/dL\n    'Sodium': (110, 165),                  # mEq/L\n    'Potassium': (2.0, 8.0),               # mEq/L\n    'Glucose': (20, 800),                  # mg/dL\n    'Albumin_Serum': (1.0, 6.0),          # g/dL\n}\n\ndef validate_ranges(df, ranges):\n    \"\"\"\n    Validate that values fall within expected ranges\n\n    Returns DataFrame with validation results\n    \"\"\"\n    validation_results = pd.DataFrame()\n\n    for col, (min_val, max_val) in ranges.items():\n        if col in df.columns:\n            valid = (df[col] >= min_val) & (df[col] <= max_val)\n            validation_results[f'{col}_valid'] = valid\n            invalid_count = (~valid).sum()\n            print(f\"{col}: {invalid_count} invalid values\")\n\n    return validation_results\n```\n\n### Logical Consistency Checks\n```python\ndef check_logical_consistency(df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Check for logical inconsistencies in lab data\n\n    Returns list of inconsistency descriptions\n    \"\"\"\n    issues = []\n\n    # eGFR should be inversely related to creatinine\n    # High creatinine should mean low eGFR\n    if 'eGFR' in df.columns and 'Serum_Creatinine' in df.columns:\n        high_cre_high_gfr = df[(df['Serum_Creatinine'] > 2.0) & (df['eGFR'] > 60)]\n        if len(high_cre_high_gfr) > 0:\n            issues.append(f\"{len(high_cre_high_gfr)} patients with high creatinine but high eGFR\")\n\n    # BUN/Creatinine ratio should be reasonable (typically 10-20)\n    if 'BUN_Creatinine_Ratio' in df.columns:\n        abnormal_ratio = df[(df['BUN_Creatinine_Ratio'] < 5) | (df['BUN_Creatinine_Ratio'] > 50)]\n        if len(abnormal_ratio) > 0:\n            issues.append(f\"{len(abnormal_ratio)} patients with abnormal BUN/Cr ratio\")\n\n    # Hematocrit should be ~3× hemoglobin\n    if 'Hemoglobin' in df.columns and 'Hematocrit' in df.columns:\n        expected_hct = df['Hemoglobin'] * 3\n        hct_mismatch = abs(df['Hematocrit'] - expected_hct) > 10\n        if hct_mismatch.sum() > 0:\n            issues.append(f\"{hct_mismatch.sum()} patients with Hct/Hgb mismatch\")\n\n    # Total cholesterol should be approximately LDL + HDL + (TG/5)\n    if all(col in df.columns for col in ['Total_Cholesterol', 'LDL_Cholesterol', 'HDL_Cholesterol', 'Triglycerides']):\n        calculated_total = df['LDL_Cholesterol'] + df['HDL_Cholesterol'] + (df['Triglycerides'] / 5)\n        total_mismatch = abs(df['Total_Cholesterol'] - calculated_total) > 50\n        if total_mismatch.sum() > 0:\n            issues.append(f\"{total_mismatch.sum()} patients with cholesterol calculation mismatch\")\n\n    return issues\n```\n\n## Data Quality Metrics\n\n### Completeness\n```python\ndef calculate_completeness(df):\n    \"\"\"Calculate completeness metrics\"\"\"\n    total_cells = df.size\n    missing_cells = df.isnull().sum().sum()\n    completeness = (1 - missing_cells / total_cells) * 100\n\n    return {\n        'overall_completeness': completeness,\n        'complete_rows': (df.notna().all(axis=1).sum() / len(df)) * 100,\n        'columns_completeness': ((1 - df.isnull().sum() / len(df)) * 100).to_dict()\n    }\n```\n\n### Validity\n```python\ndef calculate_validity(df, validation_ranges):\n    \"\"\"Calculate percentage of valid values per column\"\"\"\n    validity_scores = {}\n\n    for col, (min_val, max_val) in validation_ranges.items():\n        if col in df.columns:\n            valid_count = ((df[col] >= min_val) & (df[col] <= max_val)).sum()\n            non_null_count = df[col].notna().sum()\n            if non_null_count > 0:\n                validity_scores[col] = (valid_count / non_null_count) * 100\n\n    return validity_scores\n```\n\n### Consistency\n```python\ndef calculate_consistency(df):\n    \"\"\"Calculate consistency metrics\"\"\"\n    consistency_metrics = {}\n\n    # Example: Check if BUN/Creatinine ratio matches calculated ratio\n    if all(col in df.columns for col in ['BUN', 'Serum_Creatinine', 'BUN_Creatinine_Ratio']):\n        calculated_ratio = df['BUN'] / df['Serum_Creatinine']\n        consistent = abs(df['BUN_Creatinine_Ratio'] - calculated_ratio) < 1\n        consistency_metrics['BUN_Cr_ratio'] = (consistent.sum() / len(df)) * 100\n\n    return consistency_metrics\n```\n\n## Clinical Lab Value Plausibility\n\n### CKD-Specific Validation\n```python\ndef validate_ckd_labs(df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Validate lab values specific to CKD patients\n    CKD patients have different \"normal\" ranges\n    \"\"\"\n    validation_results = {}\n\n    # In CKD, expect:\n    # - Elevated creatinine\n    # - Reduced eGFR\n    # - Often anemia (low hemoglobin)\n    # - Possibly elevated potassium, phosphorus\n    # - Possibly low calcium, bicarbonate\n\n    if 'eGFR' in df.columns and 'Serum_Creatinine' in df.columns:\n        # For CKD patients, eGFR should be <60\n        ckd_stage_3_plus = df['eGFR'] < 60\n        elevated_creatinine = df['Serum_Creatinine'] > 1.2\n\n        # These should generally align\n        aligned = ckd_stage_3_plus == elevated_creatinine\n        validation_results['eGFR_creatinine_aligned'] = (aligned.sum() / len(df)) * 100\n\n    # Check for anemia (common in CKD)\n    if 'Hemoglobin' in df.columns:\n        anemic = df['Hemoglobin'] < 13  # Simplified threshold\n        validation_results['anemia_prevalence'] = (anemic.sum() / len(df)) * 100\n\n    return validation_results\n```\n\n### Temporal Consistency (for longitudinal data)\n```python\ndef check_temporal_plausibility(df, date_col, value_col, max_change_per_day):\n    \"\"\"\n    Check if changes over time are physiologically plausible\n\n    Args:\n        df: DataFrame with datetime column and value column\n        date_col: Name of datetime column\n        value_col: Name of value column\n        max_change_per_day: Maximum plausible change per day\n    \"\"\"\n    df_sorted = df.sort_values(date_col)\n\n    # Calculate change per day\n    df_sorted['days_diff'] = df_sorted[date_col].diff().dt.days\n    df_sorted['value_diff'] = df_sorted[value_col].diff()\n    df_sorted['change_per_day'] = df_sorted['value_diff'] / df_sorted['days_diff']\n\n    # Identify implausible changes\n    implausible = abs(df_sorted['change_per_day']) > max_change_per_day\n\n    return df_sorted[implausible]\n```\n\n## Data Harmonization Quality Checks\n\n### Pre-Harmonization Assessment\n```python\ndef pre_harmonization_check(df, exclude_cols=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Assess data quality before harmonization\n\n    Returns dictionary with quality metrics\n    \"\"\"\n    if exclude_cols is None:\n        exclude_cols = []\n\n    metrics = {}\n\n    # Check for mixed decimal formats (commas vs periods)\n    for col in df.columns:\n        if col not in exclude_cols:\n            str_values = df[col].astype(str)\n            has_commas = str_values.str.contains(',', na=False).sum()\n            has_periods = str_values.str.contains(r'\\.', na=False, regex=True).sum()\n\n            if has_commas > 0 and has_periods > 0:\n                metrics[f'{col}_mixed_decimals'] = True\n\n    # Check for scientific notation\n    for col in df.columns:\n        if col not in exclude_cols:\n            str_values = df[col].astype(str)\n            scientific = str_values.str.contains('e|E', na=False, regex=True).sum()\n            metrics[f'{col}_scientific_notation_count'] = scientific\n\n    # Check for missing values\n    metrics['missing_by_column'] = df.isnull().sum().to_dict()\n    metrics['rows_with_missing'] = df.isnull().any(axis=1).sum()\n\n    return metrics\n```\n\n### Post-Harmonization Validation\n```python\ndef post_harmonization_check(df, expected_format='X.XX'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Validate data after harmonization\n\n    Args:\n        df: Harmonized DataFrame\n        expected_format: Expected format (e.g., 'X.XX' for 2 decimal places)\n    \"\"\"\n    issues = []\n\n    # Check all values are numeric (as strings)\n    for col in df.columns:\n        if col == 'patient_id':\n            continue\n\n        # Check format\n        str_values = df[col].astype(str)\n\n        # Check for scientific notation\n        scientific = str_values.str.contains('e|E', na=False, regex=True).sum()\n        if scientific > 0:\n            issues.append(f\"{col}: {scientific} values still in scientific notation\")\n\n        # Check for commas\n        commas = str_values.str.contains(',', na=False).sum()\n        if commas > 0:\n            issues.append(f\"{col}: {commas} values still have commas\")\n\n        # Check decimal places\n        if expected_format == 'X.XX':\n            # Should match pattern like \"12.34\"\n            import re\n            pattern = r'^\\d+\\.\\d{2}$'\n            correct_format = str_values.str.match(pattern, na=False).sum()\n            total_valid = df[col].notna().sum()\n\n            if correct_format < total_valid:\n                issues.append(f\"{col}: {total_valid - correct_format} values not in X.XX format\")\n\n    return issues\n```\n\n## Best Practices for Clinical Data Quality\n\n### 1. Always Validate Before and After Processing\n```python\n# Before processing\nprint(\"Pre-processing quality check:\")\npre_metrics = pre_harmonization_check(df)\n\n# Process data\ndf_clean = clean_data(df)\n\n# After processing\nprint(\"Post-processing quality check:\")\npost_issues = post_harmonization_check(df_clean)\n```\n\n### 2. No conversion needed if threshold is None\n    if threshold is None:\n        return value\n\n    conversion_factor = rules['conversion_factor']\n\n    # Special handling for HbA1c\n    if feature_name == 'HbA1c' and rules.get('special_conversion'):\n        if value > threshold:  # In mmol/mol\n            # Convert mmol/mol to %\n            return (value / 10.929) + 2.15\n        return value\n\n    # For most tests: if value suggests SI units, convert to US units\n    # Pattern 1: Value too high -> divide by conversion factor\n    if threshold and value > threshold and conversion_factor > 1:\n        # Likely in SI units, convert to US\n        return value / conversion_factor\n\n    # Pattern 2: Value too low -> multiply by conversion factor\n    if threshold and value < threshold and conversion_factor > 1:\n        # Likely in SI units, convert to US\n        return value * conversion_factor\n\n    return value\n\n\ndef clean_numeric_string(value_str):\n    \"\"\"\n    Clean a numeric string: handle scientific notation, European decimals, etc.\n\n    Args:\n        value_str: String representation of a number\n\n    Returns:\n        Float value or NaN if conversion fails\n    \"\"\"\n    if pd.isna(value_str):\n        return np.nan\n\n    # Convert to string\n    value_str = str(value_str).strip()\n\n    # Handle empty strings\n    if value_str == '' or value_str.lower() == 'nan':\n        return np.nan\n\n    # Replace European decimal comma with period\n    value_str = value_str.replace(',', '.')\n\n    # Remove any non-numeric characters except decimal point, minus sign, and scientific notation\n    # Keep: digits, period, minus/plus, 'e' or 'E'\n    import re\n    value_str = re.sub(r'[^\\d.eE+\\-]', '', value_str)\n\n    # Try to convert to float\n    try:\n        return float(value_str)\n    except (ValueError, TypeError):\n        return np.nan\n\n\ndef harmonize_lab_data(input_file, output_file):\n    \"\"\"\n    Main function to harmonize clinical lab data\n\n    Args:\n        input_file: Path to input CSV file\n        output_file: Path to output CSV file\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"Reading data from {input_file}...\")\n    df = pd.read_csv(input_file)\n\n    print(f\"Initial data shape: {df.shape}\")\n    print(f\"Initial missing values: {df.isnull().sum().sum()}\")\n\n    # Step 1: Clean all numeric columns (convert strings to floats)\n    print(\"\\nStep 1: Cleaning numeric strings...\")\n    numeric_cols = [col for col in df.columns if col != 'patient_id']\n\n    for col in numeric_cols:\n        df[col] = df[col].apply(clean_numeric_string)\n\n    print(f\"After cleaning, missing values: {df.isnull().sum().sum()}\")\n\n    # Step 2: Remove rows with any missing values\n    print(\"\\nStep 2: Removing incomplete records...\")\n    rows_before = len(df)\n    df = df.dropna()\n    rows_after = len(df)\n    print(f\"Removed {rows_before - rows_after} rows ({(rows_before - rows_after)/rows_before*100:.2f}%)\")\n    print(f\"Remaining rows: {rows_after}\")\n\n    # Step 3: Apply unit conversions\n    print(\"\\nStep 3: Applying unit conversions...\")\n    conversion_counts = {}\n\n    for col in numeric_cols:\n        if col in UNIT_CONVERSION_RULES:\n            # Track how many values are converted\n            original_values = df[col].copy()\n            df[col] = df[col].apply(lambda x: convert_value(x, col))\n\n            # Count conversions (significant changes)\n            changes = abs(df[col] - original_values) > 0.01\n            conversion_counts[col] = changes.sum()\n\n    print(\"\\nConversions applied:\")\n    for col, count in sorted(conversion_counts.items()):\n        if count > 0:\n            print(f\"  {col}: {count} values converted\")\n\n    # Step 4: Validate ranges\n    print(\"\\nStep 4: Validating ranges...\")\n    out_of_range_summary = {}\n\n    for col in numeric_cols:\n        if col in UNIT_CONVERSION_RULES:\n            rules = UNIT_CONVERSION_RULES[col]\n            min_val = rules['min']\n            max_val = rules['max']\n\n            out_of_range = ((df[col] < min_val) | (df[col] > max_val)).sum()\n            if out_of_range > 0:\n                out_of_range_summary[col] = out_of_range\n\n    if out_of_range_summary:\n        print(\"WARNING: Values outside expected physiological ranges found:\")\n        for col, count in sorted(out_of_range_summary.items()):\n            print(f\"  {col}: {count} values out of range\")\n        print(\"These may be extreme clinical values or remaining unit issues.\")\n    else:\n        print(\"All values within expected physiological ranges!\")\n\n    # Step 5: Round to 2 decimal places\n    print(\"\\nStep 5: Rounding to 2 decimal places...\")\n    for col in numeric_cols:\n        df[col] = df[col].round(2)\n\n    # Step 6: Format as strings with exactly 2 decimal places\n    print(\"\\nStep 6: Formatting to X.XX format...\")\n    for col in numeric_cols:\n        df[col] = df[col].apply(lambda x: f'{x:.2f}' if pd.notna(x) else x)\n\n    # Likely in SI units, convert to US\n        return value / conversion_factor\n\n    # Pattern 2: Value too low -> multiply by conversion factor\n    if threshold and value < threshold and conversion_factor > 1:\n        # Likely in SI units, convert to US\n        return value * conversion_factor\n\n    return value\n\n\ndef clean_numeric_string(value_str):\n    \"\"\"\n    Clean a numeric string: handle scientific notation, European decimals, etc.\n\n    Args:\n        value_str: String representation of a number\n\n    Returns:\n        Float value or NaN if conversion fails\n    \"\"\"\n    if pd.isna(value_str):\n        return np.nan\n\n    # Convert to string\n    value_str = str(value_str).strip()\n\n    # Handle empty strings\n    if value_str == '' or value_str.lower() == 'nan':\n        return np.nan\n\n    # Replace European decimal comma with period\n    value_str = value_str.replace(',', '.')\n\n    # Try to convert to float\n    try:\n        return float(value_str)\n    except (ValueError, TypeError):\n        return np.nan\n\n\ndef harmonize_lab_data(input_file, output_file):\n    \"\"\"\n    Main function to harmonize clinical lab data\n\n    Args:\n        input_file: Path to input CSV file\n        output_file: Path to output CSV file\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"Reading data from {input_file}...\")\n    df = pd.read_csv(input_file)\n\n    print(f\"Initial data shape: {df.shape}\")\n    print(f\"Initial missing values: {df.isnull().sum().sum()}\")\n\n    # Track how many values are converted\n            original_values = df[col].copy()\n            df[col] = df[col].apply(lambda x: convert_value(x, col))\n\n            # Count conversions (significant changes)\n            changes = abs(df[col] - original_values) > 0.01\n            conversion_counts[col] = changes.sum()\n\n    print(\"\\nConversions applied:\")\n    for col, count in sorted(conversion_counts.items()):\n        if count > 0:\n            print(f\"  {col}: {count} values converted\")\n\n    # Step 5: Round to 2 decimal places\n    print(\"\\nStep 5: Rounding to 2 decimal places...\")\n    for col in numeric_cols:\n        df[col] = df[col].round(2)\n\n    # Step 6: Format as strings with exactly 2 decimal places\n    print(\"\\nStep 6: Formatting to X.XX format...\")\n    for col in numeric_cols:\n        df[col] = df[col].apply(lambda x: f'{x:.2f}' if pd.notna(x) else x)\n\n    # No conversion needed if threshold is None\n    if threshold is None:\n        return value\n\n    conversion_factor = rules['conversion_factor']\n\n    # Special handling for HbA1c\n    if feature_name == 'HbA1c' and rules.get('special_conversion'):\n        if value > threshold:  # In mmol/mol\n            # Convert mmol/mol to %\n            return (value / 10.929) + 2.15\n        return value\n\n    # For most tests: if value suggests SI units, convert to US units\n    # Pattern 1: Value too high -> divide by conversion factor\n    if threshold and value > threshold and conversion_factor > 1:\n        # Likely in SI units, convert to US\n        return value / conversion_factor\n\n    # Pattern 2: Value too low -> multiply by conversion factor\n    if threshold and value < threshold and conversion_factor > 1:\n        # Likely in SI units, convert to US\n        return value * conversion_factor\n\n    return value\n\n\ndef clean_numeric_string(value_str):\n    \"\"\"\n    Clean a numeric string: handle scientific notation, European decimals, etc.\n\n    Args:\n        value_str: String representation of a number\n\n    Returns:\n        Float value or NaN if conversion fails\n    \"\"\"\n    if pd.isna(value_str):\n        return np.nan\n\n    # Track how many values are converted\n            original_values = df[col].copy()\n            df[col] = df[col].apply(lambda x: convert_value(x, col))\n\n            # Count conversions (significant changes)\n            changes = abs(df[col] - original_values) > 0.01\n            conversion_counts[col] = changes.sum()\n\n    print(\"\\nConversions applied:\")\n    for col, count in sorted(conversion_counts.items()):\n        if count > 0:\n            print(f\"  {col}: {count} values converted\")\n\n    # Step 4: Validate ranges\n    print(\"\\nStep 4: Validating ranges...\")\n    out_of_range_summary = {}\n\n    for col in numeric_cols:\n        if col in UNIT_CONVERSION_RULES:\n            rules = UNIT_CONVERSION_RULES[col]\n            min_val = rules['min']\n            max_val = rules['max']\n\n            out_of_range = ((df[col] < min_val) | (df[col] > max_val)).sum()\n            if out_of_range > 0:\n                out_of_range_summary[col] = out_of_range\n\n    if out_of_range_summary:\n        print(\"WARNING: Values outside expected physiological ranges found:\")\n        for col, count in sorted(out_of_range_summary.items()):\n            print(f\"  {col}: {count} values out of range\")\n        print(\"These may be extreme clinical values or remaining unit issues.\")\n    else:\n        print(\"All values within expected physiological ranges!\")\n\n    # Save to output file\n    print(f\"\\nSaving harmonized data to {output_file}...\")\n    df.to_csv(output_file, index=False)\n\n    print(f\"\\nFinal data shape: {df.shape}\")\n    print(\"Harmonization complete!\")\n\n    #
